Autonomous LLM agents promise to speed this up by running the loop on their own, but it is still unclear whether they can do this in industry-scale systems rather than on benchmarks or small proxy tasks. We build human-on-the-loop machine learning engineer (MLE) agents that propose, implement, train, and evaluate model changes on large-scale search rankers. Engineers retain control over objective design, search direction, and final-model review. We address these with parallel training runs, an objective calibrated to match online impact, and careful context engineering. In one campaign, the system ran 1,097 completed candidate experiments and accepted 325, a 29.6\% acceptance rate compared with 15.6\% in the earlier campaign under a different objective and search constraint. Its best model increases search volume by 0.23\% in an A/B test of large scale. Several improvements were architectural changes the team had never tried, drawn from the broader industry and literature, suggesting that MLE agents can explore regions of the design space that human intuition overlooks. We find that the design of the harness around the model matters: intuition suggests that a stronger underlying model is enough, but in our experience, the design of the loop around it is just as critical. A System 1 model answers a multiple-choice question in a single pass. Flan-PaLM and Med-PaLM 2 report 57.6% and 72.3% on MedMCQA under a different protocol, so ours is a cheap, fast baseline and not a competitive system. ##### **Anticipating or Speculating? Making Futures Pay in AI Ethics**
2611.22268v2 by John P. Nelson

Philosophers have recently been trying to develop uniform decision rules for instances in which physicians' judgments diverge from the recommendations of medical artificial intelligence (AI) systems. I argue that this effort is fatally flawed because the performance and appropriate use of algorithmic recommendation systems by physicians are highly context-specific, empirical medical matters rather than epistemological ones. In such instances, general decision rules can be stipulated only to the extent that they abstract away from relevant, indeed determinative, contextual circumstances. The alleged problem of physician-AI disagreement, and others like it, play into longstanding disputes in technology ethics about the viability and utility of envisioning possible futures: is this useful "anticipation" or wasteful "speculation"? I suggest that the two may be distinguished. Anticipation explores plausible futures to help inform present actions under irreducible uncertainty. Speculation abstracts away uncertainty, developing formal solutions to stipulated circumstances whose relationship to the real present is often quite unclear. Efforts to develop crisp philosophical solutions to physician-AI disagreement, among other problems, fall into the latter category. Technology ethics should remember that fruitful futures thinking must both begin from and return to the messy, uncertain present.

##### **Not All Evidence Is Admissible: Evidence Governance for Tool-Augmented Medical Agents**
2611.22169v1 by Runxun Zhang, Onofrio Antonio Catalano, Jinqiao Wang, Jingwei Wei

Tool-augmented medical agents treat whatever their tools return as evidence. We show that this default is costly: medical tools fail silently, and a frozen reasoner cannot ignore what enters its context, so many cases it would have answered correctly are flipped to wrong once tool outputs are added. We call this evidence hijacking. $\mathcal{V}$-information explains the phenomenon: the reasoner has lost the option to ignore, so a tool output can carry negative usable information, and moving the decision of what to keep outside the reasoner restores that option. The theory further shows that part of any filtering gain comes merely from letting the reasoner see less, and only selection by content lets it see better. Building on this, we recast tool-augmented reasoning as evidence governance, which separates deciding what counts as evidence from the reasoner that answers, and propose context-independent evidence governance (CIEG), the first medical agent framework that rules, after acquisition, on whether each individual tool output may enter as evidence. Every tool output is written as a card that can be checked against the raw input; an independent checker that sees only the input and the cards decides which cards become evidence; and the reasoner answers from the admitted cards alone, with no training anywhere. On medical question answering, fundus glaucoma screening, and chest X-ray pneumonia classification, it achieves the highest accuracy among models of comparable scale, and significantly outperforms state-of-the-art tool-using agents and multi-agent systems built on the same backbone at lower cost.

##### **A Generative AI-Driven Architecture for Whole-Home Intelligent Services: Edge Models, Memory Management, and Household Applications**
2611.21232v1 by Mo Zhou

Generative artificial intelligence is entering the home. Using the home local server as a unifying organizing axis, this article develops a design-oriented review framework. Sources from arXiv, CNKI, ACL Anthology, and official documentation were searched through November 2026 and coded into five evidence categories: reviews, system prototypes, benchmarks, HCI and eldercare studies, and official documentation. System designs were mapped onto shared architectural modules, identifying nine recurrent responsibilities of the home local server. Generative models support perceptual interpretation, intent understanding, and candidate planning, while deterministic validation, knowledge-graph constraints, and authorization leases confine candidate actions to an inspectable space. Home control, emotional companionship, and intelligent guarding can be compared within a common framework: they share state and memory but differ in latency, false positive and negative rates, proactivity, and permissions. Existing benchmarks rely on virtual environments and cannot substitute for real-deployment evaluation. Local deployment is not inherently safer, and technical continuity alone does not establish the social or medical effects of companionship and guarding. The article proposes a five-dimensional evaluation framework covering architectural capability, system resources, privacy, safety and reliability, and human experience, together with six design principles and a future research agenda for local-first intelligent home systems.

#### Abstracts
##### **StereoVLM: Binocular Vision-Language Models for Metric 3D Spatial Reasoning**
2611.27915v1 by Chi Yan, Guoyang Zhao, Wentao Yuan, Yixiao Ge, Yanning Zhou, Dan Xu

Vision-language models (VLMs) exhibit strong semantic understanding but remain limited in their ability to reason about metric 3D space in the physical world. Existing VLMs typically infer 3D scene structure from one or more images without known camera calibration or cross-view geometric constraints. This makes metric 3D inference inherently ambiguous and leaves it unclear whether their spatial predictions are grounded in visual evidence or driven primarily by semantic priors. In contrast, calibrated stereo provides explicit cross-view geometry that resolves this ambiguity and offers a more reliable foundation for metric 3D perception and geometric generalization. We investigate how VLMs can learn transferable 3D representations directly from calibrated binocular observations. To this end, we introduce StereoVLM, the first geometry-aware binocular VLM for metric 3D spatial reasoning. StereoVLM follows a hierarchical learning paradigm spanning from cross-view correspondence and disparity estimation to calibration-conditioned metric depth perception and semantic spatial reasoning. We further find that disparity supervision generalizes substantially better than direct binocular depth supervision on unseen environments. To evaluate these capabilities, we introduce Stereo3D-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark across three levels, from point-level geometry to object-level spatial reasoning. StereoVLM-8B achieves 95.17% depth accuracy ($δ_1$) and a 66.98 average object-level score on Stereo3D-Bench. Intervention results further indicate that the model's performance on Stereo3D-Bench benefits from valid cross-view geometry and cannot be explained solely by additional visual tokens or semantic priors. Hierarchical geometric supervision also yields average gains of 3.54 points across five spatial benchmarks and 4.3 percentage points in RoboCasa manipulation success.

摘要：

##### **Decision Columns as an Index: Interpretable Embeddings for Explainable Entity Matching and Search**
2611.27910v1 by Aadharsh Kannan, Robert Bruckner, Philip A. Bernstein

Business data systems must determine whether records refer to the same entity and retrieve records satisfying requests whose decisive properties are implicit. As agents act on these records, both tasks require auditable reasons. Dense embeddings perform well but offer opaque coordinates, cannot be filtered by meaning, and require corpus re-encoding to add new properties. We propose interpretable embeddings built from decision columns: each column stores a decision model's calibrated answer to a natural-language question, with outcomes yes, no, or not stated. A record's embedding is its answers across an ordered set of questions. Similarity decomposes exactly into per-question contributions, and new questions add coordinates without altering existing ones.
  On public product benchmarks, question-space retrieval matches dense candidate generation statistically in a retrieve-then-verify entity matcher, while providing exact explanations for accepted matches. Questions derived from retrieval failures triple mean reciprocal rank without re-encoding prior answers. For search, inferring the attributes of a suitable result outperforms encoding only the literal request, and combining question-space and dense scores improves retrieval, though not at our prespecified confidence threshold. A single small neural network predicts the full interpretable vector from an existing dense embedding, enabling corpus backfilling from a few thousand labeled rows at roughly 1/200 the cost. We position interpretable embeddings as a complementary, readable retrieval channel for explaining matches, filtering on attributes, and extending indexes as failures expose missing facts.

摘要：

##### **ChildAI-Gov: An Evidence-Linked AI System for Assessing EdTech Policies Against Children's Digital Rights**
2611.27799v1 by Naveen Sigalapalli, Huizhi Liang, Clara Crivellaro

We present ChildAI-Gov, an explainable AI system for assessing whether education-technology (EdTech) policies respect children's digital rights. Generative AI is reaching children's education faster than its governance can keep pace, yet checking a tool against children's rights remains manual, expert-dependent, and slow. We propose the Child Rights Impact Score (CRIS), an evidence-linked measure across five dimensions: safety, privacy, transparency, autonomy, and non-discrimination. ChildAI-Gov reads an EdTech tool's privacy or AI policy and scores each dimension by combining a large-language-model judgement (gpt-4o-mini) with a deterministic rule check. A retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) component grounds every score in the governing provisions, retrieved from a curated knowledge base of children's rights frameworks (the EU AI Act, UNICEF guidance, and others), so each score is tied to the evidence behind it and an auditor can trace which provision it rests on. We also construct AIPolicy-EdTech, a dataset of 57 real EdTech policies. Mistral-7B computed 29% of 100 financial calculations correctly against 100% for Python, a live quote cut price error from 210% to 0.00%, and hybrid retrieval reached 76% Hit@5 on 50 filing questions. Yet in a live case study, both agent answers passed validation while describing AMD sample data under NVIDIA's and Alphabet's names. A controlled upper-bound study on the FinRank benchmark explains why: when one figure in the shared evidence is corrupted, self-consistency stays confident (agreement 0.55 to 0.59 while accuracy falls from 0.50 to 0.00), and a shared-evidence check (the kind our validator performs) catches 35% of the errors, whereas a check against the original passage catches 77% ($p = 5.96 \times 10^{-8}$); for DeepSeek-R1-8B, 68% vs. 90%. Verification must be independent of the evidence the writer saw.

##### **Auditing black-box clinical prediction models with a large language model through queries alone: a blinded test with planted flaws**
2611.24877v1 by Zhiyu Liu, Xinyu Yang

Large language models (LLMs) are used to explain clinical prediction models, on the promise that they can say what a model computes, find its faults and even reveal new knowledge in it. We tested the three promises where the answers could be checked. From the International Stroke Trial we trained four kinds of model (logistic regression, explainable boosting machine, gradient-boosted trees, neural network) for two outcomes on patients randomised earlier, planted one flaw in each of 24 copies (an under-predicted subgroup, a reversed risk factor or an implausible step) and mixed them with the 8 clean models. GPT-6 Astra audited each model three times, blind, through a tool that only returned the model's predictions; the judging rules were committed before any run and applied by program. Its surrogate models, with a median of 128 terms, reproduced the audited models' log-odds with a mean $R^2$ of 0.976 (0.959 for an additive surrogate of 42 terms fitted by an analyst). It found the planted flaw in 69 of 72 runs (96%; 95% CI 80% to 99%); a script written for the purpose found all 24. It also reported many more problems: 60% of 1,089 checkable reports failed the pre-specified check against the model's average behaviour; post hoc, most of these were real but small steps, and 15% of the checkable reports were not found in the model. Of 601 unexpected relationships it read out of the clean models, 11% held in patients randomised later (random pairs 6%; a regression scan's shorter list of its strongest candidates, 28%); after a check on the training data, 33% against 30%. With query access alone, an LLM can describe a black-box model faithfully and find nearly every planted flaw, as a purpose-written script also did; its reports of problems must be checked on the model and its discoveries on data.

##### **What Survives the Shift from Text to Pixels? Conditional Protection against Prompt Injection in Image-Rendered Documents**
2611.24231v1 by Haoyang Liu, Yinong Xu, Zhe Lang, Zhenyu Guan, Qianyun Zhang

Multimodal language models increasingly answer questions over external documents, exposing them to indirect prompt injection: commands planted in untrusted content to hijack the response. Rendering untrusted content as page images lowers attack success on average, leaving open which injected operations survive rendering, at what utility cost, and whether failed image attacks reflect unread commands or commands read but not followed. We pair text and page-image versions of 191 English question-answering sources across six models, measuring marker appending, answer formatting, and answer replacement alongside clean Question-Answering utility. Rendering reduces append and format execution for every model, yet Qwen3-VL-8B still executes image-borne replacement at 77.96\%. Protection also depends on target type, rendering settings, wording, and repetition, and a search over eight fixed attack candidates largely closes the image deficit in appending and formatting for Qwen and Gemini, though not for GPT-5-nano. Utility ratios on original questions range from 93.31\% to 101.61\%, but Qwen falls to 61.99\% when answers combine two evidence spans. Illegibility cannot explain all failed attacks: models often transcribe and identify image-borne commands they do not execute, and trusted user authorization raises execution on unchanged pages. Inside Qwen, patching early attack-region states induces all three operations, more weakly in images. Attention blocking suggests why: a text command acts through its own positions, whereas visual encoding spreads an image command and its target across the instruction page. Rendering thus offers conditional protection, motivating evaluations that report security by model, operation, and attack budget alongside utility.

##### **How Reward Gain Imbalance Shapes Ranking: Rethinking Rubric-Based Reward Aggregation**
2611.24005v1 by Zhengyuan Fan, Zhonghua Wu, Tianyu Cheng, Qun Chen

Rubric-based reinforcement learning constructs rewards for aligning large language models with human preferences, typically by equally aggregating continuous scores that a judge assigns to individual criteria. However, scoring heterogeneity across criteria can distort candidate rankings. Existing work has focused on improving judges, learning criterion weights, or refining aggregation, whereas the interaction between scoring heterogeneity and ranking remains insufficiently understood. We formalize this heterogeneity as \emph{reward gain imbalance}: each criterion enters the aggregated reward with its own reward gain, the gap between its expected scores on satisfied and unsatisfied responses. Counterintuitively, our experiments show that removing this imbalance, even with ground-truth labels, lowers ranking accuracy. To explain this, we derive an exact decomposition of the reward difference between any two candidates into a base satisfaction signal, a reward gain imbalance term, and a residual term, showing that reward gain imbalance can attenuate or amplify the base signal, and finding that misrankings usually concentrate on pairs where it attenuates. Guided by this analysis, we propose the adaptive \textbf{D}ual-\textbf{C}enter \textbf{C}lipping aggregation (DCC), which bounds each criterion's contribution to the reward difference instead of simply removing the imbalance. Using only the scores of the current candidate group, it determines a clipping interval that imposes a common upper bound on per-criterion score differences between candidates, limiting the excessive influence of extreme scores while preserving continuous judgment information....

##### **DL101 The Representation Capability of Neural Network Models**
2611.24980v1 by Fernando Berzal

Universal approximation theorems (Cybenko, Funahashi, Hornik & Leshno) provide the mathematical justification for why neural networks are powerful enough to model complex, nonlinear phenomena. Shallow networks can approximate any continuous function on compact sets. Leshno's theorem clarified that non-polynomial activations are necessary for universality, which explains why ReLU, logistic, hyperbolic tangent, and other common activations work (but pure polynomials do not).
  However, approximation theorems provide no efficiency, training, nor generalization guarantees. They do not indicate how many neurons are needed for a given accuracy, whether gradient descent will find the right parameters, or whether networks trained on finite data will generalize well.
  Barron provided quantitative error bounds for particular function classes (approximation rates for functions with bounded Fourier norm), whereas Montúfar was the first to show that expressivity grows exponentially with depth (in terms of the number of linear regions). Deep networks are strictly more expressive than shallow ones with the same number of parameters. Depth is fundamentally more powerful than width, providing a rigorous mathematical explanation for the use of deep learning models.

##### **Done, But Still Working: Anytime Evaluation of When Coding Agents Should Stop**

##### **JET: Just-Enough Exploration Tuning for Efficient Tool-Calling Agents in Repository-Level Question Answering**
2611.22539v1 by Yoonho Choi, Taehyeon Han, Seonghyeon Lee

In repository-level question answering (QA), an agent explores a code repository with tools and should answer a question once enough information is gathered. Moreover, even though existing methods improve task performance through supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on expert trajectories, SFT also propagates the over-exploration in the trajectories to the agent. ##### **Right Labels, Wrong Reasons: Signed Feature Attribution for Multi-Label Explainability**

##### **Anticipating or Speculating? Making Futures Pay in AI Ethics**
2611.22268v2 by John P. Nelson

Philosophers have recently been trying to develop uniform decision rules for instances in which physicians' judgments diverge from the recommendations of medical artificial intelligence (AI) systems. I argue that this effort is fatally flawed because the performance and appropriate use of algorithmic recommendation systems by physicians are highly context-specific, empirical medical matters rather than epistemological ones. In such instances, general decision rules can be stipulated only to the extent that they abstract away from relevant, indeed determinative, contextual circumstances. The alleged problem of physician-AI disagreement, and others like it, play into longstanding disputes in technology ethics about the viability and utility of envisioning possible futures: is this useful "anticipation" or wasteful "speculation"? I suggest that the two may be distinguished. Anticipation explores plausible futures to help inform present actions under irreducible uncertainty. Speculation abstracts away uncertainty, developing formal solutions to stipulated circumstances whose relationship to the real present is often quite unclear. Efforts to develop crisp philosophical solutions to physician-AI disagreement, among other problems, fall into the latter category. Technology ethics should remember that fruitful futures thinking must both begin from and return to the messy, uncertain present.

##### **Not All Evidence Is Admissible: Evidence Governance for Tool-Augmented Medical Agents**
2611.22169v1 by Runxun Zhang, Onofrio Antonio Catalano, Jinqiao Wang, Jingwei Wei

Tool-augmented medical agents treat whatever their tools return as evidence. We call this evidence hijacking. $\mathcal{V}$-information explains the phenomenon: the reasoner has lost the option to ignore, so a tool output can carry negative usable information, and moving the decision of what to keep outside the reasoner restores that option. The theory further shows that part of any filtering gain comes merely from letting the reasoner see less, and only selection by content lets it see better. Building on this, we recast tool-augmented reasoning as evidence governance, which separates deciding what counts as evidence from the reasoner that answers, and propose context-independent evidence governance (CIEG), the first medical agent framework that rules, after acquisition, on whether each individual tool output may enter as evidence. Every tool output is written as a card that can be checked against the raw input; an independent checker that sees only the input and the cards decides which cards become evidence; and the reasoner answers from the admitted cards alone, with no training anywhere. On medical question answering, fundus glaucoma screening, and chest X-ray pneumonia classification, it achieves the highest accuracy among models of comparable scale, and significantly outperforms state-of-the-art tool-using agents and multi-agent systems built on the same backbone at lower cost.

The explainer sees one request and one decision, never the policy, and has thirty turns in which it may call the policy on altered requests and ask whether a candidate set of facts is sufficient. ##### **Programmable Semantic Interventions: Learning Causal Carriers from Behavioral Programs**
2611.21307v1 by Paolo Muratore, Mackenzie Weygandt Mathis

Causal abstraction explains a frozen network by aligning its activations with the variables of a simpler, interpretable model, and checking that interventions on the two agree. Finding such an alignment currently requires a counterfactual dataset: for every pair of prompts, the answer the network ought to give once the variable has been changed. In practice, those labels are produced by an executable algorithm for the behavior, written before studying the model supposed to implement it. However, the criterion these methods optimize only leverages the answer an intervention should produce. Here, we develop an approach that lets the investigator define that relationship directly: the answers the variable can produce, and which of them an intervention should yield. We call this interface a semantic program and introduce Programmable Semantic Interventions (PSI): a low-rank activation subspace learned so that interchanging it changes the model's answer as the program declares, with the expected answers read from the frozen model itself. On the MIB benchmark PSI performs on par with supervised alignment at matched data and training budget, without curated pairs, and it needs no ground-truth labels; while on natural science questions with gold labels withheld, it tracks the model's own answers better than a subspace trained on gold labels. ##### **Effects of Conventional, Adaptive, and AI-Powered Upscaling on the Legibility of License Plates**
2611.18949v1 by Hany Farid, Joshua D. Bucci, Emily A. Cooper

Small details in digital images -- a face, a street sign, a license plate -- are routinely upscaled in hopes of making them interpretable. Upscaling options now range from conventional interpolation, whose operations are spatially invariant and fully explainable, to AI-powered methods that synthesize detail potentially absent from the source. Here, we ask which of these techniques most reliably helps human readers. In two online experiments, we measured legibility of 255 US license plates ranging in resolution from 128 to 8 pixels tall, as well as their legibility after upscaling by eight different methods. Legibility of the un-upscaled plates collapsed from ceiling to floor across a narrow band of plate heights (32 to 10 pixels). Conventional upscaling techniques (bilinear, bicubic, and Lanczos interpolation) gave modest gains of about 5-9 percentage points within this range. Nearest-neighbor interpolation, however, was harmful, rendering unreadable many plates that were readable without any upscaling. Advanced methods diverged: SwinIR, an open-source AI enhancer, raised performance by up to 38 points relative to no upscaling, while an adaptive denoiser-based image prior and commercial AI upscaler tended to impair legibility. We found no strong evidence of consistent character hallucinations across methods. Decomposing the task, conventional interpolators mainly helped readers count characters, whereas SwinIR alone made the characters themselves more identifiable.

##### **Dancing Tokens on the Vocabulary Network from LLM Generation**
2611.18248v1 by Yuan-Chao Hu

Large language models (LLMs) generate text one token at a time, yet how a particular generation relates to the geometry learned in pre-training remains unclear. The fundamental explainability of LLMs is elusive. Here, we build a similarity network from the input embeddings of six open LLMs and map generation onto it. The token-embedding network is scale-free while heavy-tailed, as are random embeddings with matched covariance, but far more fragmented. Distillation barely changes this topology because input embeddings are largely inherited. The knowledge space is thus deterministic from pre-training. Consecutive generated tokens are rarely graph neighbours and, in most models, no more often than in shuffled order, so generation is not a walk on the predefined network, although the tokens it visits are concentrated within it. Through the logit lens in a layer- wise manner, tokens settle late in depth in most models, and repetitive models remain confined to small domain-specific sub-graphs. These observables could serve as inference-time monitors of degeneration. We thus provide a fresh perspective on understanding LLM generation in the microscopic way from node or link prediction by graph learning.

##### **The Transformer Paradigm: Dynamic Processing and a New Principle of Inference**
2611.17953v1 by Marco Giunti, Fabrizia Giulia Garavaglia

We argue that the Transformer revolution is not adequately understood as a merely quantitative expansion of deep learning, but as the emergence of a new paradigm based on a different principle of inference. We interpret the Transformer as a system in which token representations are transformed by parameterized transformations, or transforming concepts. During inference, some of these transformations are dynamically constructed from the current input: previously acquired transformations are first adapted and then applied. At the mechanical-structural level, this dynamic processing is realized through output-weight interconnections, whereby outputs of some networks determine the weights of others. The resulting mode of processing, which we call Sequence-level Interactive Dynamic Parallel Processing (SIDPP), provides a framework for explaining strong prompt sensitivity and opens research directions concerning interpretability, control, alternative AI architectures, neuromorphic systems, and possible biological realizations of dynamic processing.

However, the opacity of AI-based decision-making in education (especially with deep learning and generative AI models) can hamper decision-making, especially when outcomes appear unjustified or incorrect. In this chapter, we argue that three key principles (uncertainty, explainability, and human responsibility) are essential for the legitimate use of AI in educational settings. Acknowledging the inherent uncertainties in AI-based decisions, analogous to those in human decision-making, is fundamental. Explainability is critical for ensuring that the functioning and outcomes of AI applications are transparent and comprehensible to both educators and learners. Furthermore, human responsibility is crucial, particularly in human decision-making, which remains indispensable across all stages of AI development and deployment. By examining these three principles, this chapter provides a comprehensive guide for stakeholders seeking to integrate AI thoughtfully and effectively into the educational system.

##### **From Data to Wisdom: Rethinking AI Through an Epistemic Lens**
2611.16726v2 by Afonso Eduardo

This paper proposes the Data-to-Wisdom Epistemic Architecture for AI (EAAI), a conceptual framework treating Data, Information, Knowledge, Understanding and Wisdom as five distinct but recursively interacting epistemic functions: observation, contextual interpretation, modeling and prediction, critical evaluation and informed judgment. Two observations motivate it: sequential readings of the Knowledge Pyramid obscure how these functions interact, and a targeted review of twelve Bayesian reference books finds that prior distributions are defined mathematically while "prior information" and "prior knowledge" are not consistently distinguished as epistemic categories. A Bayesian interpretation relates the likelihood, prior and posterior distributions, posterior prediction, model checking and decision analysis to the five functions, without limiting EAAI to Bayesian systems. Extending "prior" in an epistemic sense, the paper distinguishes five kinds of prior context that can shape the model, the likelihood, the prior, evaluation and action. Three worked applications -- quantitative financial risk, actuarial modeling in health insurance and agentic AI for medical reimbursement claims -- show how EAAI structures the analysis of model assumptions, evidence interpretation, validation, dependencies and decision consequences, separating the prediction of a risk, the appraisal of its reliability and the justification of action. It draws implications for AI and machine learning, including evaluation by function, treating intermediate model outputs as evidence open to challenge and separating generation from appraisal, and compares EAAI with work on DIKW, Bayesian workflow, AI evaluation and risk governance. Its contributions are an epistemic vocabulary with recursive relationships, a more accessible reading of Bayesian methodology, a typology of prior context and clearer methodological terminology

The uniqueness claim is conditional; for $k<d_z-1$, where $d_z$ is the number of world states, this limit makes its premise unsatisfiable, so the claim has no content there. The experiments that motivate it leave the assumption: a caption sees only part of an image, and the paper states no experiment meets its hypotheses exactly. The theorem does not reach those experiments, and for $k<d_z-1$ its idealized same-state limit is vacuous. One of the paper's four quantitative results survives: a gap-free lower bound. Its bound vanishes only at perfect scene-explained fraction; it bounds alignment error but cannot certify good-view alignment. A canonical frame is determined only up to a rotation and a latent permutation. Paired samples fix the relative frame at effective rank $k$; a global rotation remains. ##### **PDO Drug Screening Data for Evaluating and Training AI Drug Response Prediction Models**
2611.15769v1 by Y. Zhu, K. Marlor, V. Lavelle, T. S. Brettin, M. Sacco, C. Siniscalchi, H. W. Raeder, M. R. Weil, R. L. Stevens

Anti-cancer drug response prediction (DRP) is critically important for precision oncology and therapeutic development. Currently, most DRP models are developed using drug screening data from immortalized cancer cell lines, which can differ substantially from patient tumors at the genomic and molecular levels, even though patient tumor response is the ultimate prediction target. To help bridge this gap and bring DRP models closer to their intended clinical application, we conducted four drug screening studies using patient-derived organoids (PDOs), which more faithfully recapitulate patient tumors than immortalized cell lines. These studies generated data for improving and evaluating DRP models, including multi-omics profiles of PDO lines, drug feature data, and treatment response measurements. Using these data, we retrained and evaluated multiple publicly available DRP models under three model-building scenarios: training models using cell line data only, training models using combined cell line and PDO data, and pretraining models on cell line data followed by fine-tuning on PDO data. We then evaluated and compared model prediction performance on PDOs across these analysis scenarios. In addition, we also assessed the consistency of drug screening viability data generated by different laboratories using different assay technologies and/or experimental protocols.

摘要：

##### **Terms of Care: Designing for Participation in Data Governance by Disability Communities**
2611.15720v1 by Madiha Zahrah Choksi, Reeda Shimaz Huda, Rita Faia Marques, Anja Thieme, Cecily Morrison

Improving disability representation in AI-generated media requires community-specific datasets, yet openly sharing such data can expose communities to misuse, decontextualization, and exploitation. We examine how disability communities can participate in dataset governance through context-sensitive licensing. Working with three disability advocacy organizations, we conducted semi-structured interviews, an educational webinar and modular license activity to promote community participation in data stewardship. Our findings show that governance in these high-context communities relies on organizational memory (especially situated harms); both formal authority and informal relational practices; communication; and resulting trust. The modular license activity made organizational data choices more accessible and values-led, allowing communities to adapt Creative Commons clauses to express permitted uses, sector-specific restrictions, safeguarding requirements, and commitments to respectful representation. Yet, concerns remain about monitoring and enforcing appropriate data use. We contribute (1) an empirical account of governance tensions, (2) a practical modular approach, and (3) design implications for AI data platforms that support relational, context-sensitive governance beyond standardized, one-size-fits-all frameworks.

摘要：

##### **A Reliability Study of Explainable AI for Freight Mode Classification**
2611.15716v1 by Ali Alawi Balfaqih, Mansur M. Arief

Machine learning models are increasingly used to support freight transportation decisions, but their predictions are rarely accompanied by evidence that the associated explanations can be trusted. In this study we investigate explainable machine learning for classifying shipments as Air/Air Charter or Truck/Ocean using the PEPFAR Supply Chain Management System Delivery History dataset, with 5,964 shipment records and 47 input features after cleaning, where freight cost and pack price are excluded as near-proxies of the transportation mode. We compare three Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP) architectures, Logistic Regression, Random Forest, and gradient boosting with imbalance-aware metrics and paired significance tests across one hundred random replications. We then evaluate the explanations themselves, namely the stability of SHAP feature rankings across retraining and across models, the faithfulness of SHAP and LIME attributions under a feature-deletion test, and the geographic structure of the destination effects, which we map for all 38 destination countries. The results suggest that the tree ensembles significantly outperform the neural and linear models (86.8% against 83.3-85.0% mean testing accuracy) while their leading features remain the most stable across retraining. Removing the five features ranked most important by SHAP or LIME changes the predicted probabilities roughly four times as much as removing randomly chosen features. The destination effects form a coherent geographic pattern that simple port access does not explain. However, models with similar accuracy disagree substantially on which features matter most, and thus explanation stability and faithfulness should be assessed before such explanations are used to support freight transport decisions.

摘要：

##### **When Agents Need to Consolidate Memories: What RRR Consolidation Explains, and What It Does Not Explain, in Conversation ESAA**
2611.15644v1 by Elzo Brito dos Santos Filho

Language-model agents can share conversational history across sessions, but persistence does not guarantee retrieval of current decisions and their provenance. This engineering study presents Memory-TCG, a Conversation ESAA extension inspired by ringing, resonance and renormalization (RRR), and an exploratory synthetic evaluation comparing chronological retrieval, two simplified baselines (B, recency-aware, and B', authority-aware), full RRR, and variants under three output budgets. With 480 facts and 1,024 tokens, full RRR recalled 0.44 of current decisions, versus 0.06 for chronological retrieval and 0.58 for the oracle ceiling; with 4,096 tokens it recalled all of them. B' and the no-resonance variant matched full RRR in decision recall across all nine scale-budget combinations, as expected when the active decision set and the adapter ordering are the same. The isolated contribution of ringing to decisions was not measured: comparator D1 cuts facts before the graph is built, and the product retains active decisions by rule. Before the output limit, full-RRR conflict F1 was 0.615 at all three scales; after it, at 480 facts and 1,024 tokens, it was 0.000. Reserving 20% of the budget for conflicts raised that F1 to 0.431 in that scenario, at the cost of lowering decision recall from 0.44 to 0.34, and to 0.930 at 4,096 tokens without decision loss. Fixtures are repetitive, comparators are simplified, and the evaluation was not independently replicated; the results support an engineering analysis, not a claim of selectionist superiority.

摘要：

##### **ProdPilot: A Layered Model Context Protocol System for Automated Production Readiness and Deployment of Vibe-Coded Projects**
2611.17381v1 by Muhammad Sudais Khalid, Muhammad Junaid Asif, Rehan Naveed Abbasi, Rana Fayyaz Ahmad

AI-assisted development environments turn natural-language requests into working applications, yet the projects they produce often fail on their first deployment for operational reasons: credentials in source files, missing security middleware, no container definition, no health endpoint and no delivery pipeline. Automated repair of such defects usually relies on free-form model generation, which varies between runs and is seldom checked independently. This paper presents ProdPilot, a local Model Context Protocol server that audits Node.js and React projects against 50 production readiness rules, repairs violations through a bounded loop, gates the result and deploys it. Each rule is classified by what its fix requires: 28 fixes are fixed templates, 16 are filled with values extracted from the project and only 6 are written by the IDE agent's model, so 88.0\% of the ruleset needs no model-written content, and across 499 real repositories 93.5\% of the violations found fall in those classes. A fix, however it was produced, is accepted only if the checker that flagged the rule now passes. In a full-chain run over 23 sample projects, template and extraction fixes that were applied as written passed their checkers in 87.6\% and 95.0\% of cases, and independent verification rejected 25 of the 237 fixes reported as applied. A deployability model trained on 684 real deployments ranks held-out projects at 0.909 ROC AUC, a gated project was deployed, smoke-tested and connected to an active CI/CD pipeline in 82 seconds, and the unchanged server worked from the IDE agents in VS Code, Cursor and Windsurf, where live agents resolved all six delegated rules on the first attempt under the same verification. ProdPilot itself runs no model.

摘要：

##### **KQML-Liver: Interpretable Hybrid Quantum Machine Learning with Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks for Liver Disease Classification**
2611.15241v1 by Nhat-Long Nguyen, Viet-Loi Dinh, Trong-Nghia Pham

Liver disease remains a major global health concern, highlighting the need for reliable and accessible early detection methods. Although recent advances in machine learning, deep learning and quantum machine learning (QML) have introduced promising diagnostic tools, many approaches suffer from limited interpretability and inflated performance caused by data leakage or overly simplistic evaluation protocols. These constraints reduce their reliability in real-world clinical practice. This paper proposes KQML-Liver, a hybrid framework that integrates QML with Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) to enhance predictive performance and interpretability. The architecture combines parameterized quantum circuits for expressive feature representation with KAN-based functional mappings that capture nonlinear relationships in an interpretable manner. Evaluated on the Indian Liver Patient Dataset (ILPD) under a rigorous preprocessing protocol preventing data leakage, the proposed model achieves 82% classification accuracy and 100% recall, detecting all positive cases, outperforming existing baselines while providing improved transparency in clinical predictions. These results suggest that hybrid KAN-quantum architectures offer a promising direction for interpretable AI-assisted medical diagnosis.

摘要：

##### **A frontier machine intelligence for human neuroimaging**
2611.15094v1 by Chenhui Zhao, Akshay Rao, Akhil Kondepudi, Yiwei Lyu, Xinhai Hou, Rikhil Seshadri, Cheng Jiang, Soumyanil Banerjee, Rushikesh Joshi, Samir Harake, Ashok Srinivasan, Shruti Mishra, Francisco Rivas-Rodriguez, Seunghong Rhee, Nathaniel Roddenberry, Neeraj Chaudhary, Zach Wilseck, Paul Park, Honglak Lee, Vikas Gulani, Aditya Pandey, Todd Hollon

Medical imaging is essential for diagnosing disease, guiding treatment and monitoring patients over time. Although artificial intelligence has advanced image interpretation, existing medical models often address restricted tasks, while general-purpose proprietary models lack access to private clinical imaging required to achieve medical expertise. Developing models that can interpret complex examinations and communicate their findings in the context of clinical decisions remains a major challenge. Here, we show that Neuro-VL, a vision-language model trained on nearly one million neuroimaging studies from routine clinical care, can interpret complete imaging examinations and engage in natural language conversations about their findings. Across internal and international datasets, Neuro-VL outperformed frontier general-purpose and specialized medical models in diagnosing, localizing, and describing abnormalities, as well as generating comprehensive radiology reports. The model also compared serial examinations to assess disease progression and more reliably resisted misleading clinical assumptions than leading frontier models from OpenAI and Google. These results extend health system learning beyond individual predictions to interactive image interpretation and longitudinal disease monitoring. They demonstrate how the collective clinical experience of health systems can be used to develop medical AI with specialized expertise and broad clinical capabilities.

摘要：

##### **Science Prediction Universe**
2611.15049v1 by Zengji Tu, Xiang Liu, Jiarui Sun, Ruoyu Wu, Zhihui Qi, Chengda Wu, Yutao Zhou, Bo Yang, Ziyan Zhang, Jialong Wu, Liang Chen, Kuan Li, Steven Dillmann, Yuanqi Du, Soojung Yang, Shizhe Diao, Jianjun Wu, Junlang Liu, Xuming Hu, Lin Chang, Baobao Chang, Xiaowen Chu, Philip S. Yu, Tong-Yi Zhang, Hao Cheng, Qin Han, Yanye Feng, Chenru Duan, Chuncheng Liu, Yan Liu, Yuchen Gao, Zhao Li, Ronghua Zhang, Tory Xu, Renjian Song, Yaokai Fu

Humanity has long sought to explain natural phenomena. Reliable prediction of experimental outcomes is a central test of our understanding of the processes that produce them. If artificial general intelligence (AGI) is to surpass human scientific capabilities, it must also predict experimental outcomes more reliably than humans. Such capabilities could accelerate scientific discovery across disciplines, help address humanity's most pressing challenges, and improve human well-being. We introduce Science Prediction Universe (SPU), a benchmark of hidden experimental outcomes with 100 public verified tasks from chemistry, materials science, biology and medicine, and 16 private tasks from unpublished wet-lab experiments. Given an experimental setup and candidate conditions, an agent works with any resources it needs to rank the candidates by a measured outcome.
  We evaluate thirteen agent configurations spanning twelve frontier models across two harnesses (Codex and Claude Code). Current agents remain far from saturating wet-lab outcome prediction, with substantial performance disparities across models: on the public benchmark, configurations separate into two distinct tiers, with task-adjusted Spearman correlations ranging from 0.227 for the lowest-performing configuration to 0.478 for the best. Analysis of 1,155 scored public trajectories (227,103 tool calls) suggests that the bottleneck lies in scientific judgment rather than tool execution: agents rely on shared monotone priors, agreeing far more with one another than with experimental reality (0.52 vs. 0.33), and frequently compute trivial physical or chemical proxies that do not influence the final outcome.

摘要：

##### **Emergent Vision-Shaped Thinking in Text-Only Reasoning through Multimodal Training**
2611.14942v1 by Qi Zhang, Bohan Wang, Qianru Sun, Hanwang Zhang

Does multimodal training shape how a language model thinks, even when the input is text only? We test this with targeted causal ablations across 10 multimodal configurations from 6 model families, with matched text-only backbones and cross-family transfer controls. We contrast spatial-imagery tasks that invite mental scene construction with symbol-manipulation tasks solvable by symbolic rules alone. We identify a vision-language axis in hidden-state space and ablate its projection at inference. In Chameleon-7B, this ablation drops accuracy on BIG-Bench Hard (BBH) navigate by 27.6 pp while leaving boolean expressions intact (+5.6 pp), a 33.2 pp double dissociation. The effect is direction-specific and layer-localized, extends to mental-simulation tasks such as mental rotation, paper folding, and perspective taking, and is not explained by task difficulty, reasoning depth, or sequential update load. Together, these results reveal emergent vision-shaped thinking (EVT): text-only reasoning that causally recruits representations introduced or strengthened by vision-language training, without assuming an image-like internal format. The measured effect is spatially concentrated at 7-8B and broadens to non-spatial visual attribute binding in the tested 32B configuration. In dual-path unified models (BAGEL, Janus-Pro), the causal handle localizes to the generation pathway as a compact one-dimensional carrier. The understanding pathway also encodes spatial information in a higher-rank, probe-decodable form, but is not the causal handle. To separate decodability from causal use, we analyze concentration, alignment, and semantic location. These diagnostics explain why behavior, probing, and intervention can diverge. Multimodal training thus reshapes the geometry of text-only reasoning, not just how visual input is processed.

摘要：

##### **Do Large Language Models Show Racial and Sex Bias in Pain Management? A Counterfactual Audit of GPT, Claude, Gemini, Llama, DeepSeek and Qwen**
2611.14935v1 by Richard J. Young, Alice M. Matthews

Racial and ethnic minority patients receive less pain treatment than non-Hispanic White patients, and large language models (LLMs) used as medical AI could reproduce that bias. Prior audits tested a few proprietary models, stated race explicitly and rarely measured the models' own response instability. In exploratory, non-preregistered analyses, Study 1 had four open-weight LLMs assess six acute-pain vignettes in every combination of four races and two sexes, with identity stated or cued only by a name (two names per group), comparing each identity with a White male reference on the same case and sampling seed. In Study 2, 24 configurations of 17 models, including OpenAI's GPT-4.1 mini and gpt-oss, Anthropic's Claude Haiku 4.5, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash, Meta's Llama, DeepSeek and Qwen, with reasoning on and off and at two precisions, answered the same vignettes and the 50 clinician-written Q-Pain cases. In this single-prompt, JSON-constrained task, no difference survived correction in Study 1, none of 1,386 comparisons survived the false discovery rate in Study 2, and every pain-severity difference was equivalent to zero within one point on a 0-10 scale (within a quarter point for 237 of 238 Q-Pain comparisons). Opioid decisions were bounded less precisely (Study 1 could not exclude differences of about 20 percentage points), so they show no detected difference, not proof of none. The few differences surviving correction within a model favoured Black patients. Models differed far more from one another and across their own settings than between patients (the reference patient received the high opioid dose in 6% to 93% of responses), although serving software, provider and prompt format also differed. Fairness audits of clinical AI should use several names per group, pair comparisons on the seed, report the noise floor and test each deployed configuration.

#### Abstracts
##### **SUAF: Spectral Universal Advantage Features for Zero-Shot Policy Improvement**
2611.27919v1 by Huaze Tang, Runqing Miao, Tianren Peng, Lam Nguyen, Chao Wang, Jairong Li, Wenbo Ding

Reward-independent representations enable reinforcement learning agents to reuse knowledge across tasks, but limited representation capacity raises three fundamental questions: what statistical dependence should these representations capture, which components of that dependence should be retained for policy improvement, and how does discarding the remaining components affect decision-making? We address these questions through canonical dependence analysis. For a reference policy, we construct an action-centered, positive-lag canonical dependence matrix (CDM) that isolates the additional predictive contribution of the current action beyond the current state. Its singular value decomposition yields Spectral Universal Advantage Features (SUAF), paired features that recover delayed advantage through linear reward projections. We show that the spectrum quantifies mutual information between current action and future state-action visitation conditioned on current state and determines reward specific approximation errors. Retaining the leading modes gives optimal rank-constrained approximations under both isotropic average-case and reward-uniform minimax criteria. Separating the immediate reward contrast also avoids the spectral floor arising from direct compression of the full advantage operator. To learn SUAF from reward-free data, we develop an action-centered Soft-HGR objective with bidirectional temporal-difference estimation. We further connect spectral approximation errors to sufficient conditions for policy improvement. Finite-state experiments validate the spectral predictions and feature-learning method, while zero-shot evaluations on discrete tasks and continuous-control benchmarks demonstrate the utility of the learned representations across rewards.

摘要：

##### **Auditable Financial Knowledge Graphs: Schema-Driven Construction and Intrinsic Quality Evaluation**
2611.27835v1 by Samarth Sarin, Lovepreet Singh, Dhagash Mehta

Financial documents combine narrative disclosures, accounting metrics, corporate structures, management commentary, and time-dependent facts, making them difficult to represent using conventional chunk-based retrieval-augmented generation systems. This paper presents an auditable, schema-driven methodology for constructing temporal financial knowledge graphs from page-delimited documents parsed using a general purpose document intelligence service. Overlapping document windows undergo phased large language model extraction of structural entities, quantitative observations, and domain-specific relationships. Candidate graph elements are validated against predefined schemas and consolidated through scope-aware exact, normalized, fuzzy, embedding-based, and LLM-assisted entity resolution. Temporal merge constraints, deterministic edge deduplication, conflict-aware property management, and fine-grained provenance preserve issuer identity, reporting-period validity, and traceability to source evidence. The contributions of this work comprise the proposed methodology for financial knowledge graph construction, the creation of an evidence-grounded temporal knowledge graph from heterogeneous financial documents, and an intrinsic framework for evaluating the quality of the resulting graph. The evaluation framework assesses seven weighted dimensions, two hard validation gates, and structural diagnostics. By separating graph-quality assessment from downstream retrieval and answer generation, the framework supports systematic diagnosis and iterative refinement of the constructed graph.

摘要：

##### **Graph Signal Generation via Graph-Aware Diffusion Models**
2611.27826v1 by Vimal K B, Sergio Rozada, Antonio G. Marques, Elvin Isufi, Hadi J. Rad

Many signals are defined on the nodes of a graph, such as traffic flow at road sensors or user purchases of related products. They are often partly observed, and learning their distribution enables imputing missing values and forecasting future ones. Diffusion models learn such distributions, but their forward noise is independent across nodes and does not depend on the graph. We introduce graph-aware diffusion (GAD), whose forward process is a heat diffusion on the graph driven by Gaussian noise. In the graph Fourier basis, the forward transition is a scalar Gaussian channel at each frequency. With a shared diffusion rate, high graph frequencies are attenuated faster than low ones. We propose Mode-Adaptive SNR Scheduling (MASS), which assigns each graph frequency its own diffusion rate and keeps all of them at the same SNR. For graph-stationary signals, MASS equalizes the normalized error of the optimal linear denoiser across frequencies. We bound reverse-SDE stiffness as a function of the (shifted) Laplacian's condition number, with a logarithmic upper bound for MASS and a linear lower bound for shared rates. GAD with MASS improves recommendation accuracy by 5-19%, reduces traffic-imputation RMSE by up to 7.8% with half as many reverse steps, and improves forecasting MAE and RMSE using only 3 reverse steps.

摘要：

##### **ChildAI-Gov: An Evidence-Linked AI System for Assessing EdTech Policies Against Children's Digital Rights**
2611.27799v1 by Naveen Sigalapalli, Huizhi Liang, Clara Crivellaro

We present ChildAI-Gov, an explainable AI system for assessing whether education-technology (EdTech) policies respect children's digital rights. Generative AI is reaching children's education faster than its governance can keep pace, yet checking a tool against children's rights remains manual, expert-dependent, and slow. We propose the Child Rights Impact Score (CRIS), an evidence-linked measure across five dimensions: safety, privacy, transparency, autonomy, and non-discrimination. ChildAI-Gov reads an EdTech tool's privacy or AI policy and scores each dimension by combining a large-language-model judgement (gpt-4o-mini) with a deterministic rule check. A retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) component grounds every score in the governing provisions, retrieved from a curated knowledge base of children's rights frameworks (the EU AI Act, UNICEF guidance, and others), so each score is tied to the evidence behind it and an auditor can trace which provision it rests on. We also construct AIPolicy-EdTech, a dataset of 57 real EdTech policies. On it, the five dimensions cohered as a single measure (Cronbach's alpha = 0.79), and grounding made scoring stricter while preserving rankings; an ungrounded baseline was far more lenient, passing nearly half the policies where ChildAI-Gov passed none. It offers evidence-linked decision support on documented commitments, not observation of real-world behaviour or determinations of legal compliance.

摘要：

##### **Search-RSI: Expanding Search Worlds Along Multimodal Agents' Capability Frontier**
2611.27780v1 by Chengchao Lv, Yin Wang, Chunzheng Zhu, Wanli Chang

Multimodal search agents answer knowledge intensive visual questions by grounding them in retrieved evidence, and recent recipes train them on multi hop tasks that are synthesized from knowledge graphs and grown online to keep pace with the agent. These recipes, however, treat the searchable world as fixed: a new question can be well formed and verifiable while its evidence is absent from the retrieval backend, so the agent issues the right query, retrieves nothing, and is penalized as if its policy were at fault. Such unreachable negatives accumulate with every round of growth. We propose Search-RSI, a recursive self improvement framework that expands the search world along the agent's capability frontier. In each round, the current policy diagnoses its partially mastered regions of a task space spanned by hop depth, relation rarity, and distractor density; new tasks there are grown together with their evidence; and the same policy decides how they are used: candidates on which its probes disagree feed reinforcement learning, while those beyond its reach become hint guided demonstrations of its own. Keeping the policy inside this loop matters: an improver frozen at the start of training loses 3.2 points. On nine multimodal search benchmarks, Search-RSI keeps improving where static task growth saturates, raising the average accuracy of the recipe it builds on by 9.8 and 9.5 points at 9B and 27B, of which 7.0 points are out of reach for a world that is merely made consistent. The loop uses no external model and only privileged task structure that is removed before training, at 1.27x the compute of static growth.

摘要：

##### **QuASAR: Toward an LLM-Assisted Framework for Screening, Prototyping, and Assessing Quantum-Advantage Opportunities in Scientific Computing**
2611.27778v1 by Mark L. Carroll, Jordan A. Caraballo-Vega, Brian P. Powell, John L. Schnase

Identifying scientific computations that could benefit from quantum algorithms requires both domain knowledge and fluency in quantum algorithm design, expertise that rarely resides in one person. We report results from QuASAR, an experimental, two-agent, LLM-driven pipeline intended to bridge this gap. One agent screens scientific literature for computational kernels with a plausible quantum-algorithmic analogue and proposes candidates, each backed by citations. A second agent independently verifies those citations. For candidates that survive verification, it reconstructs the source's computational structure, formulates a quantum mapping, builds and executes a prototype, and benchmarks it against classical baselines. We applied this pipeline to ten candidate computations drawn from the astrophysics literature. Nine candidates were carried through to a small-scale, benchmarked prototype. We report three in detail, chosen as the clearest examples with the least redundancy across the set. They span combinatorial optimization, stochastic simulation, and Bayesian inference. The pipeline attempted to supply quantum-design expertise a domain scientist might lack, reconstructing source algorithms where one existed and building original, disclosed mappings where none did. None of the three case studies achieved quantum advantage at the tested scales, and their astrophysical framing has not yet undergone expert validation. What the experiment does show is that the pipeline can turn a published idea into a runnable prototype, which a scientist can then inspect, test against classical methods, and decide whether to pursue. QuASAR is an exploratory framework, offered to the community for evaluation, critique, and further development, with an approach that could broaden the use of quantum computing in scientific research and extend to computational improvements more generally.

摘要：

##### **AutoOntologyBench: A Task-Grounded Benchmark for Agentic Knowledge Engineering**
2611.27734v1 by Minye Shao, Chenghao Xiao, Xingyue Zhao, Yang Long

Foundation-model agents are increasingly extending beyond software engineering (SWE) into autonomous scientific research, where acquiring and integrating domain knowledge remains a critical challenge. Recent industrial advances have increasingly adopted knowledge graphs and ontology-based semantic layers to organize heterogeneous knowledge into reusable representations. We ask: Can autonomous agents construct ontologies that help them solve real tasks, and do they offer measurable benefits over alternative knowledge representations? Existing benchmarks evaluate ontology correctness or agent task success in isolation, leaving the value of autonomously acquired knowledge unresolved. We therefore introduce AutoOntologyBench, a task-grounded benchmark comprising 19,947 audited tasks grounded in 18 real-world knowledge sources across 7 domains, with source-native verification. Spanning Application, Repair, Organization, and Construction, AutoOntologyBench compares nine frontier-model agents under matched knowledge representations. Layered on retrieval, agent-built ontologies add 6.1 points of task success, yet trail an expert-derived reference by 13.0, identifying autonomous knowledge acquisition as the bottleneck to scalable scientific research. Code and resources are available at https://github.com/VinyehShaw/AutoOntologyBench.

摘要：

##### **Spatiotemporal Graph Neural Networks for Fault Detection and Structural Learning in Chemical Processes: Use Case on the Tennessee Eastman Process**
2611.27698v1 by Rayane Ammar Khodja, Alexandre Voisin, Victor Costa, Fanny Casteran, Benoit Celse, Benoît Iung

Classical Fault Detection and Diagnosis (FDD) methods, including many data-driven approaches, assume a static normal operating space and interpret deviations from a fixed reference as fault indicators. When Operating Conditions (OCs) vary over time, this assumption breaks down: legitimate transitions trigger false alarms while moderate faults go undetected. We propose a spatiotemporal Graph Neural Network (GNN) framework that decomposes the normal operating space into OC-specific subspaces linked by transition functions, with a dual learning objective combining reconstruction loss and a Deep SVDD one-class term. It learns adjacency matrices through Graph Attention Networks (GATs) and integrates spatial modelling with temporal encoding to represent process dynamics under evolving OCs. This paper evaluates the framework's foundational components (fault detection, GATv2 spatial graph learning and temporal encoding) on the Tennessee Eastman Process (TEP) benchmark, training only on fault-free data. The architecture achieves competitive detection from reconstruction error alone, with similarity-based feature selection improving both accuracy and graph structure diversity. We then evaluate the physical interpretability of the learned attention matrices against 22 ground-truth sensor pairs derived from the TEP control structure and process topology. GATv2 attention fails to recover all known physical pairs across multiple hyperparameter configurations, suggesting a structural limitation of reconstruction-driven attention rather than a tuning issue. This challenges a common assumption in GNN-based FDD: that learned attention weights provide a basis for fault diagnosis and root-cause analysis. The architecture detects faults effectively, but the learned graph does not encode the physical topology needed for interpretable diagnosis, motivating physics-informed graph construction.

摘要：

##### **Embodied-Harness: Towards Recursive Self-Improvement for Robotic Manipulation**
2611.27689v1 by Zhenxuan Fan, Zhuoya Wang, Yuqian Yuan, Wenqiao Zhang, Juncheng Li, Jun Xiao, Siliang Tang, Yueting Zhuang

Vision-language model (VLM)-based agents offer a promising approach to robotic manipulation, yet remain limited by inadequate memory organization, uncertain failure attribution, and limited reuse of execution experience. We propose Embodied-Harness, a dual-agent framework that improves manipulation by refining the execution harness without updating model parameters. The Actor interacts with the environment through a robot-control interface, while the Reflector converts execution evidence into reusable operational guidance. Hierarchical Memory Architecture maintains short-term execution context and long-term experiential knowledge to support execution and reflection. Evidence-based Experience Reflection grounds failure diagnosis in observed interactions, while Recursive Harness Self-Improvement refines operational guidance across successive attempts. Experiments on RoboTwin 2.0, RoboSPA, and RMBench demonstrate the effectiveness of Embodied-Harness in generalization under environmental variations, spatial reasoning, long-horizon planning, and memory-dependent manipulation. Our method achieves success rates of 82.4%, 76.7%, and 94.4% on the three benchmarks, respectively. Real-world evaluation further yields an average success rate of 96.7% across three manipulation tasks. These results highlight the potential of Embodied-Harness to improve robotic manipulation through experience-driven harness self-improvement.

摘要：

##### **Learning in Symbiosis: Heterogeneous Federated Learning with ANNs and SNNs**
2611.27686v1 by Manh V. Nguyen, Liang Zhao, Bobin Deng, Xinyue Zhang, Shaoen Wu

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs), widely regarded as the third generation of neural networks, represent a promising neuromorphic computing paradigm with the potential to reduce the energy footprint of Artificial Intelligence (AI). Their en-ergy efficiency makes SNNs particularly attractive for resource-constrained Edge AI devices, motivating growing research interest in Federated Learning (FL) with SNNs. Despite these advantages, integrating SNNs into existing FL systems remains challenging, as replacing all conventional Artificial Neural Network (ANN) devices with SNN-based alternatives is impractical. Instead, we envision a gradual transition toward neuromorphic Edge AI, where ANN and SNN devices coexist and collaboratively participate in FL. To facilitate this transition, we propose Hybrid Neuromorphic Federated Learning (HNFL), a framework that enables joint training of heterogeneous ANN and SNN devices within an FL environment. Through experiments on CIFAR-10 using both VGG9 and ResNet architectures, we demonstrate the mutual benefits of collaborative ANN-SNN training. Specifically, HNFL achieves an average improvement of 16.85% when incorporating SNN devices into ANN-based FL and 8.02% when incorporating ANN devices into SNN-based FL. Furthermore, HNFL produces sparser spiking activity in the trained SNN models, with reductions of 12.4% for SResNet and 22.4% for SVGG9. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work to demonstrate that ANNs and SNNs can coexist and mutually benefit from collaborative training in FL, providing a practical path toward the gradual integration of neuromorphic computing into existing Edge AI systems.

摘要：

##### **Auditing Post-Training Unlearning in Diffusion Language Models using Sparse Autoencoders**
2611.27652v1 by Yunha Seo, Changhoon Kim

Machine unlearning is an approach to privacy protection: it aims to remove the influence of designated data while preserving the model's behavior on information that should be retained. While a growing body of work investigates this objective in autoregressive large language models, machine unlearning remains underexplored in diffusion language models (DLMs). DLMs generate text through iterative denoising, exposing partially masked states whose internal representations change throughout the generation process. This raises a DLM-specific question: can selective forgetting improve by identifying forget-data-associated features and adapting their suppression across denoising stages? We conduct a controlled investigation of this question in LLaDA-8B-Instruct. To evaluate the unlearning of knowledge plausibly encoded during pre-training, we use WMDP as our forget-domain benchmark. Using top-k sparse autoencoders and denoising-state replay, we compare static and stage-adaptive feature suppression. We find substantial shifts in forget-data-associated features across denoising stages, but tracking these dynamics alone does not reliably improve the forget-retain trade-off at the evaluated operating points. Together, our study provides an initial empirical foundation for developing and evaluating machine unlearning as a post-training privacy mechanism in DLMs.

摘要：

##### **Bringing AI Assistance to the Beznau Nuclear Power Plant: A Hierarchical System of LLM Agents for Reactor Monitoring**
2611.27556v1 by Wenqi Zhou, Leonardo Baldo, Edwin Kolbe, Manuel Arias Chao

Beznau Unit 1 in Switzerland, in operation since 1969, is the most experienced nuclear reactor still running worldwide. Five decades of incremental modernization have left its monitoring systems, alarm logs, variable catalogs, technical specifications and investigation reports in separate silos that were never designed to be queried together. As a result, investigating a core instrumentation anomaly means slow, manual gathering of knowledge scattered across databases, documents, and experienced colleagues. We present a hierarchical multi-agent assistant built on large language models (LLMs) to support these investigations and reduce the need for tedious manual searches in SAP systems. An orchestrating agent breaks down an engineer's question and hands the parts to specialized sub-agents, each with read-only access to one source. Domain expertise is written as natural-language procedures that plant engineers can read, review and revise. The system is strictly advisory, with every answer traced back to its sources. We also report organizational lessons from introducing agentic AI into a culture built on safety, traceability and conservative change. Beyond the technical architecture, the Beznau case study examines how an assistant can be introduced into a setting where evidence must be traceable, changes carefully reviewed, and operational decisions retained by plant personnel, paving the way for the responsible use of agentic AI in industrial plant monitoring.

摘要：

##### **Fix Where It Fails: Root-Cause-Guided Agentic Harness Optimization for Scene Graph Generation**
2611.27529v1 by Jee Eun Kim, Donghyeon Choi, Sangjune An, Hae Beom Lee, Kyong Hwan Jin

Scene graph generation (SGG) aims to detect objects in an image and predict the relationships between them. The standard PredCls, SGCls, and SGDet settings show how much performance drops without ground-truth boxes and labels, but not where or why a model fails. Knowing this would allow fixing a model where it fails, without retraining. We propose RoHa-SGG (Root-cause-guided Harness optimization for SGG), which extends agentic harness optimization to SGG. It optimizes the harness, the inference code around a frozen SGG model. RoHa-SGG has two main LLM agents. A root-cause analysis (RCA) agent finds the components where the model fails and diagnoses why, and a proposer agent searches for a harness that fixes these causes. We evaluate RoHa-SGG on Visual Genome and PSG with representative two-stage, one-stage, and multimodal-LLM SGG models. The analysis shows that the components where ground-truth triplets are lost differ across models and datasets. RoHa-SGG improves every metric on all five model-dataset combinations. Graph-constrained R@100 and mR@100 rise by up to 15.8 and 26.4 points for the multimodal LLM and by up to 3.6 and 10.1 points for the two-stage and one-stage models. With root-cause guidance, the harness search reaches the final score of an unguided search up to three times faster.

##### **Alignment without Transfer: Tokenization Impacts Cross-lingual Representations during Multilingual Language Model Pretraining**
2611.27491v1 by Jonathan von Rad, Pontus Stenetorp, Yao Lu

Tokenization represents one of the most crucial steps in mulilingual NLP pipelines. Yet, tokenizers routinely under-allocate vocabulary to low-resource languages. While improved allocation leads to better performance, it remains poorly understood how tokenization shapes cross-lingual representations and transfer during pretraining. To address this, we pretrain 39 mono- and bilingual 1B decoder-only models on FineWeb2-HQ across English, German, French, Arabic and Chinese, varying only the tokenizer. Text-to-SQL systems increasingly rely on context banks: curated collections of example queries, metric definitions, and domain knowledge that tell a model how an organization writes its queries. Yet correctness is often loosely defined: a question seldom fixes how an entity is counted or which table defines it, so two entries can each pass review yet resolve the same decision in opposite ways. We call this condition context bank rot. A second question follows: does a system that uses a bank generalize from it, or only reproduce it? Existing benchmarks cannot tell, as they do not control how far a test question sits from the context. We answer both questions by comparing queries decision by decision, with a fast scorer distilled from human-calibrated LLM judges. Turned inward, the comparison diagnoses and repairs a bank. Fine-tuning on a cleaned BIRD-train bank beats a same-size random subset at every epoch (+2.6 points on average), and 92.4% of our removals had their SQL corrected by an independent expert audit, against 45.6% of audited entries. In-context examples that follow one convention beat those following the opposite one by 13.3 points, while a mix is barely better than none. Turned outward, the comparison generates test questions at a controlled distance from a bank, a pipeline we call CAT (Context-Aware Testing). On CAT-BIRD, for retrieval and fine-tuning alike, the benefit of a bank falls step by step with distance and is largely gone once about three quarters of a question's logic is new, and on CAT-Spider the benefit of retrieval likewise shrinks with distance.

In-context segmentation (ICS) offers the promise of bypassing this high cost by segmenting a target video using a few annotated references, which are the sole source of surgical knowledge for a frozen model. However, existing ICS methods tend to draw such references at random, which concentrate on a few views that dominate a surgical video and can miss other objects of interest (e.g., transient instruments and small structures). To address these challenges, we propose INCISE, a training-free ICS framework for surgical videos. INCISE selects unlabeled frames from multiple surgical videos that jointly cover the local appearances of a target video, and then segments the target video using these references. With only 1.7% of training frames selected and annotated as references and no target video frame labeled, INCISE obtains 67.2% mean mIoU and outperforms the best SOTA fine-tuned method by 9.2 points. Each region is a specific group of agent functionalities. Long-term memory is essential for personalized agentic systems that interact with users over long time periods, yet current architectures only capture what happened while leaving the reasoning of why it happened to be derived later by underlying LLMs. These events can, however, convey not only what a user experienced but also how that experience shaped their behavioral patterns. We introduce STARK, a dual-graph memory architecture which treats these events not just as nodes but also as edges between behavioral insights. STARK maintains an Episodic Graph which records interactions as temporally, causally, and semantically linked episodes; and an Insight Graph which represents LLM-extracted behavioral signals scored by how many events support them, connected by the very events that led to them. At inference, behavioral knowledge is retrieved directly from insight nodes grounded by event-as-edges, eliminating repeated episodic traversal. Evaluations show that STARK improves by 1.48 judge score on LongMemEval information extraction and 0.93 on LoCoMo open domain questions over state of the art. ##### **DARSY: A Tool for Data-Driven Abstraction and Policy Synthesis for Stochastic Systems with Unknown Dynamics**
2611.25654v1 by Thom Badings, Ibon Gracia, Mahdi Nazeri, Morteza Lahijanian, Alessandro Abate

The tool is fully automated and constructs a finite interval Markov decision process (IMDP) abstraction from sampled transitions, whose probability intervals are obtained via statistical techniques and Lipschitz reasoning. Robust dynamic programming on this IMDP then yields a policy together with a lower bound on the probability that the unknown system satisfies a desired specification. This lower bound holds with a user-specified confidence; that is, it is probably approximately correct (PAC). The tool is implemented in Python and supports general Lipschitz-continuous system dynamics and specifications expressed in syntactically co-safe LTL (scLTL), which includes reach-avoid and finite-horizon safety specifications as special cases. Beyond standard IMDPs, DARSY implements a two-layer IMDP (2IMDP) abstraction that also bounds the probability of transitioning to aggregated state blocks, yielding smaller abstraction errors than IMDP abstractions and, as a result, tighter satisfaction guarantees. An open-vocabulary segmentation module converts these labels into a dense, real-time risk map, which conditions two pre-trained RL policies responsible for agile locomotion and local navigation, effectively coupling high-level VLM reasoning with low-level physical control. Real-world experiments showed the system's ability to autonomously navigate a wide range of complex real-world environments. The robot completes kilometer-long hikes with minimal intervention, interprets signs to avoid danger, and evaluates its environment to identify safe areas on which to step. This work represents a step toward the reliable deployment of systems in unstructured environments. However, it also underscores the challenges that remain in VLM reliability and spatial reasoning, indicating that foundational models are a crucial component but not yet a complete solution for navigation in the wild.

However, existing approaches merely rely on dense pixel-level observations, which forces the model to absorb redundant information while overlooking relations among task-relevant entities. Consequently, small errors may accumulate over long imagined rollouts and gradually corrupt the predictions. In this paper, we explore world models that imagine over sparse state abstractions, which poses two key challenges: 1) how to remove redundant information and form a compact state abstraction; 2) how to incorporate the relational structure to learn reliable long-horizon dynamics. To address these challenges, we propose Embodied Graph, a relational state abstraction that supports structured dynamics modeling and long-horizon imagination, and the Relational World Model (RWM), which performs imagination directly over graph states. RWM consists of (i) a Task-Aware Embodied Graph Constructor that filters redundant details and maintains graph memory through online updates, and (ii) an Embodied Graph Recurrent State Space Model that learns node-wise latent dynamics while capturing inter-node dependencies. The imagined graph states provide predictions of action consequences to guide decision-making. Experiments on Minecraft and Crafter demonstrate the effectiveness of RWM in embodied environments. Notably, in Minecraft, RWM is the only method that obtains diamonds within the given budget. Experiments on web interaction further illustrate the applicability of relational state prediction to the decision making process of web agents.

摘要：
